Then, from Lord Sadashiva, who is in formless-form, the Nada form emerged as Shastra forms. When Bindu, the first tattva of Shuddha Maya, is moved by the divine grace of Lord Shiva, Nada appears. That Nada is called Omkara and Kutila. From this, the 50 aksharas, starting with A, arise. Then, from these aksharas, padas (words) arise. Then, shastras arise. This is the general method of how shastras arise. To explain this in a specific way, it will be clear to us if we say that Lord Shiva, assuming the form of Sadashiva, manifests the Vedas and Agamas. The form of Sadashiva refers to a body made of knowledge. God performs actions by mere संकल्प (resolve). However, this form of Sadashiva assumed by Lord Shiva is not a body connected to Ashuddha Maya like our body. The body assumed by the Lord is a pure body formed by mantras. The Kamika Agama calls this body Vidya Deham. The Ajita Agama calls it Sabda Brahma Rupam. From such a Lord Sadashiva, the Vedas and Agamas emerge. The Lord manifests them. Sadashiva Murti has five faces and ten hands. Each face has three eyes. In the right hands, He holds a trident, vajra, sword, axe, and bestows the abhaya mudra. In the left hands, He holds a snake, noose, gem, fire, and goad. He wears a snake as a sacred thread. He has a jata-makuta adorned with the crescent moon. He has a serene appearance. Thus, the Kamika Agama describes the form of the Supreme Being Sadashiva. Among the five faces of Lord Sadashiva, the upward-facing face is called Ishana. This face is beautiful, clear, crystal-like, possesses pure light, and has a childlike quality. Among the five divine functions, this face performs the act of grace (anugraha). The eastward-facing face is called Tatpurusha. It has the color of molten gold, is majestic, serene, and has the appearance of early youth. This face performs the function of concealment (tirodhana) among the five divine functions. The southward-facing face is called Aghora. It is dark in color, has furrowed brows, a terrifying appearance, and protruding fangs. This face, with trembling lips, is in the prime of early youth. Among the five divine functions, it performs the act of destruction. The northward-facing face is called Vama. It has the radiance of new coral, shines with curly hair like a swarm of dark bees, is captivating to all, appears like Parvati's face, and has creeper-like eyebrows. Among the five divine functions, this face performs the act of preservation (sthiti). Now, the westward-facing face is called Sadyojata. It appears with the radiance of the moon, possessing royal characteristics, adorned with lunar ornaments, and having unwavering eyes. Among the five divine functions, this face performs the act of creation (srishti). Although these five faces bear the names Ishana and so on, in reality, the five, namely Ishana, Tatpurusha, Aghora, Vamadeva, and Sadyojata, are referred to as the Pancha Brahma Mantras in the Siva Agamas. Among these, the Ishana mantra has five kalas, the Tatpurusha mantra has four kalas, the Aghora mantra has eight kalas, the Vamadeva mantra has thirteen kalas, and the Sadyojata mantra has eight kalas. The five kalas of the Ishana mantra are the five heads of Sadashiva Murti. The four kalas of the Tatpurusha mantra are the faces of the Lord. The eight kalas of the Aghora mantra are His heart, neck, shoulders, navel, stomach, back, and chest. The thirteen kalas of the Vamadeva mantra are the private parts, thighs, knees, ankles, upper hips, hips, and two sides. The eight kalas of the Sadyojata mantra are the legs, hands, nose, crown of the head, and shoulders. Thus, the body of Lord Sadashiva is perfectly formed by the five mantras beginning with Ishana. That is why it is said that this body, this Sadashiva body assumed by the Lord, should not be thought of or considered like our body. The Linga form we see in the sanctum sanctorum of all temples is this Sadashiva form we have just contemplated. If God did not assume such a mantra-filled body, there would be no way for meditation, puja, and so on to take place. If meditation and Shiva puja do not take place, there is no way for the multitude of souls to attain salvation. Therefore, the Kirana Agama states that the formless Supreme Being Shiva assumes the form of Sadashiva only to bestow grace upon the multitude of souls. Thus, the Supreme Being Shiva, having taken form, resolves to create the shastras solely for the reason that all souls should attain liberation. Now, we will continue to contemplate how the primal texts, the Vedas and Agamas, which are called shastras, originate from Lord Sadashiva. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 16 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Periya Puranam, Part 224, Mummaiyal Ulaganda Sarga, Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. We contemplated that the Supreme Being Shiva, in His essential state (Swarupa Nilai), assumed the form of Sadashiva out of compassion, intending to reveal the primal texts, the Vedas and Sivagamas, for the salvation of souls. The Supreme Lord, who resolved to create the shastras to unite all souls with Himself, assumes the form of Sadashiva. Now, resolving to create the Vedas and Sivagamas, from His eastward-facing face, the Tatpurusha face, He brought forth the Rig Veda with 21 shakhas. From His southward-facing face, the Aghora face, He created the Yajur Veda with 101 shakhas. From His westward-facing face, the Sadyojata face, He brought forth the Sama Veda with 1000 shakhas. From His northward-facing face, the Vamadeva face, He manifested the Atharva Veda with nine shakhas. We saw that there are only two types of texts, Veda and Agama. Lord Sadashiva thus brought forth the four divisions of the Vedas from His four faces. Now, let us see how Lord Sadashiva brought forth the Agamas, which are the second type of primal texts. What we have just seen is the form of Sadashiva Murti assumed by the Supreme Being Shiva. Similarly, the Lord exists and assumes the form of Maha Sadashiva Murti. Maha Sadashiva Murti has 25 faces. That is, we already saw that Sadashiva Murti has five faces. Maha Sadashiva Murti has 25 faces, with five faces for each of the five faces of Sadashiva Murti. From the five faces within the Ishana face of this Maha Sadashiva Murti, the Lord brings forth 28 Agamas. From the Sadyojata face belonging to the Ishana face of Maha Sadashiva Murti, the five Agamas – Kamikam, Yogajam, Chintyam, Karanam, and Ajitam – emerged. Maha Sadashiva Murti brought forth the five Agamas – Deeptam, Sukshmam, Sahasram, Amshuman, and Suprabhedam – from the Vamadeva face belonging to His Ishana face. Maha Sadashiva Murti brought forth the five Agamas – Vijayam, Nisvasam, Svayambhuwam, Analam, and Veeram – from the Aghora face, one of the five faces in His Ishana face. Maha Sadashiva Murti brought forth the five Agamas – Rauravam, Makutam, Vimalam, Chandragyanam, and Bimbam – from the Tatpurusha face belonging to His Ishana face. Now, Maha Sadashiva Murti brought forth the eight Agamas – Protkritam, Lalitam, Siddham, Santanam, Sarvoktam, Paramesvaram, Kiranam, and Vatulam – from the Ishana face, one of the five faces belonging to His Ishana face. Thus, Maha Sadashiva Murti manifested a total of 28 Agamas from His Ishana face: 20 Agamas (five each from the other four faces belonging to His Ishana face) and eight Agamas from His Ishana-Ishana face. We have seen that the Supreme Being Shiva, assuming the form of Maha Sadashiva Murti, thus brought forth the primal texts, the Vedas and Sivagamas, out of compassion for souls and to bestow grace upon them. Now, let us also contemplate how these Agama shastras reach souls. Lord Sadashiva, realizing that any shastra would only be excellent if it came through a lineage of instruction, created ten Sivas by His mere thought and bestowed upon them the ten Sivagamas from Kamika to Suprabheda.

He imparted this through His Sadyojata face. Then, He made the 18 Rudras, starting with Anadi Rudra, who are in the Ishvara Tattva, knowledgeable and imparted to them the 18 Agamas from Vijaya to Vatula through His Aghora face, Tatpurusha face, and Ishana face. Those who received this instruction are of two types: Sivas and Rudras. Therefore, the first ten Agamas, that is, from Kamika to Suprabheda, are called Sivabheda because they were imparted to the Sivas. The other 18 Agamas, that is, from Vijaya to Vatula, are called Rudrabheda because they were imparted to the Rudras. Thus, the 28 Agamas are divided into two types: 10 Sivabheda Agamas and 18 Rudrabheda Agamas. However, it is very important for us to note that there is no hierarchy among the Agamas. Thus, Lord Sadashiva imparted each Agama individually to the ten Sivas, starting with Pranava, and to the 18 Rudras, starting with Anadi Rudra. Sivagnana Munivar and Sivagra Yogigal have mentioned in their respective commentaries on Sivagnana Bodham that there are also two methods of imparting this instruction. Furthermore, it is said that when the Sivagnana Shastras, also known as Siva Agamas, are transmitted through a lineage of instruction, six types of relationships arise. We said that Lord Sadashiva imparted 10 Agamas, called Sivabhedas, to 10 individuals. From them, three individuals each later received instruction. So, a total of 30 people. Now, we saw that He imparted 18 Agamas to 18 Rudras, called Rudrabhedas. These 18 individuals instructed another person. So, 18 times 18 is 36. Adding the 30 who received instruction in Sivabheda, a total of 66 people thus heard the Agamas, as stated in Tirumantiram, Payiram 57, composed by Tirumoolar Devanayanar. Now, Lord Sadashiva imparted the Agamas to Anantesvara. Anantesvara instructed Srikanta Rudra. Srikanta instructed Nandi Bhagavan. Nandi Devar instructed the Devarishis. The Devarishis instructed the Manudarishis. The Manudarishis instructed the spiritually mature among humans. Thus, the primal texts, the Vedas and Agamas, revealed by God, reach souls through the Lord's unique great compassion. Thus, Goddess Ambika, who rules the world, heard all the clear truths of the Agamas from Lord Shiva and bestowed them upon souls. For, is not Ambika the very form of the Lord's power of grace? Have we not seen that the Lord's grace, His desire to bestow grace upon souls, is Shiva Shakti? Thus, on Mount Kailash, Lord Shiva sits as the Acharya, imparting the Agamas, and Ambika hears all the clear truths of the Agamas from the Lord and bestows them upon us. Thus, we have contemplated the explanation for Sekkizhar Peruman's phrase "the clear truth of the Agamas," based on the commentary written by Sivakavimani Ayya and various other texts. Thus, we have contemplated the explanation for the 50th verse in Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam, which is "Velli Mal Varai Kailayil Veetrirundu Aruli Thulluvar Punal Veniyar Arul Seyya Thozhudu Thellu Vaimaiyin Agamathiran Ellam Theriya Ullavaru Kettu Arulinal Ulagai Aludaiyal." Now, the glorious 51st verse in Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. "Ennil Agamam Iyambiya Iraivan Tham Virumbum Unmai Yavathu Poosanai Ena Uraithu Arula Annnalar Thamayi Archchanai Puriya Atharithal Pennil Nallavalayina Perum Thavakkolundu." This is the 51st verse in Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. Its direct meaning is: Lord Shiva, who revealed countless Agamas, declared that what He desires is only puja. Understanding this, Goddess Uma, who is the best among women and like a great ascetic sprout, desired to perform puja to Lord Shiva. This is the meaning of this verse. Here, the verse is structured as "Ennil Agamam Iyambiya Iraivar Tham." "Ennil Agamam" means Agamas that have been measured and established. We already saw that there are 28 Agamas in total. Similarly, there are 207 Upagamas. Each of the 28 Moola Agamas contains many crores of granthas. Therefore, "Ennil Agamam" here can also mean countless Agamas, in the sense of number. Alternatively, "Ennil" can be split as "En nil," meaning Agamas that reside within one's thought or concept. Now, the meaning of the word "Agama." Saiva elders explain the word "Agama" by splitting it into "A," "Ga," and "Ma," which respectively denote Sat, Sat-Asat, and Asat, referring to the three entities: Pati, Pasu, and Pasam. The meaning is that Agama confirms these three entities – Pati, Pasu, and Pasam – and leads souls to salvation. The text Pingala Matam states that the name "Agama" originated from the combination of the first letters of the three words: Agatam, Gatam, and Matam. "Agatam" refers to its emanation from Lord Shiva. "Gatam" refers to its being heard by Goddess Uma. "Matam" refers to its propagation in the world as the doctrine of Shiva devotees. Furthermore, "Agama" also means "that which has come." From where has it come? It has come from the Supreme Aptar (trustworthy one). That Aptar is Paramasivan, the creator of all worlds. It means it has come from His divine tongue. Moreover, as we have already seen, "A" signifies Pasam, "Ga" signifies Pasu, and "Ma" signifies Pati. Therefore, the name Agama came about because it specifically explains these three: Pati, Pasu, and Pasam. They also explain it this way. Furthermore, some texts explain that "A" signifies Jnana (knowledge), "Ga" signifies Moksha (liberation), and "Ma" signifies the destruction of Anava Mala (egoic impurity). Thus, Agama is the text that removes impurities through knowledge and bestows liberation. As we saw above, the Lord who revealed countless Agamas, that is, the Lord who expounded them, tells Ambika that what is most dear to Him is Shiva Puja. Therefore, Ambika also desired what was dear to her Lord, Shiva. A woman desires and does what her Lord desires. That is the characteristic of virtuous womanhood. Our Ambika is the best among women. Our Umaiyamma is the best among all who are called women. Therefore, when her Lord declared that what He desires is Shiva Puja, she also desired to perform Shiva Puja. Ambika, who thus desired to perform Shiva Puja, is praised by Sekkizhar Peruman as "Perum Thavakkolundu." "Perum Thavakkolundu" means one who is like the sprout of great penance. What does that mean? It refers to a state that has resulted from penance, will perform many more penances, and will fully establish the penance performed, and further, will cause penance to flourish in the world. Therefore, Ambika's performance of penance and Shiva Puja signifies the flourishing of Shiva Puja and penance in this world.

This indicates the idea that it should flourish. God said that Shiva Puja is most dear to Him, and hearing this, Ambika also desired to perform Shiva Puja. We saw that Ambika heard what God said, didn't we? By saying "hearing," the act of learning is also included within it. By Sekkizhar Peruman's phrase "heard and received grace as it is," the meaning of "flawlessly" is conveyed here. Therefore, the secrets of the Agamas are to be learned, and by "desired to perform archana," it is implicitly understood that one should act accordingly. In accordance with the Kural, "What is the use of learning if one does not worship the good feet of the Pure One?" Ambika, who learned the Agamas from God through the process of instruction, resolved in her heart to abide by the Agama knowledge she had acquired. Therefore, she desired to perform Shiva Puja. Thus, Sekkizhar Peruman has placed this verse, which conveys that Lord Shiva told Ambika that what He truly desires is Shiva Puja, and that Ambika, thinking that what her Lord Shiva liked was also what she desired, wished to perform Shiva Puja, as the 51st verse in Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. Texts elaborately describe the Goddess as "51 Akshari." Parasakti herself embodies the 51 aksharas as her form. Thus, this verse, which speaks of what Ambika, known as "51 Akshari," desires, is placed as the 51st verse in Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam by Sekkizhar Peruman. This divine arrangement is also good evidence for the authenticity of Periya Puranam. Furthermore, the truth that Lord Shiva shines within the waves of Nada and that the benefit of knowing letters (education) is Shiva Puja is also mentioned here. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 17 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 225, Mummaiyal Ulaganda Sarga, Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. Lord Shiva stated that Shiva Puja performed according to the Agamas is most dear to Him, and hearing this, the Goddess also desired to perform puja according to the Agamas. Now, the next verse, the 52nd verse. "Nangai ulnirai kadhalai nokki nayakan thiruvullathu magizhnthe Angan eithiya muruvalum thondra aduthathu enkol ninpal ena vinava Ingu natha nee mozhintha agathin iyalinai unai archchanai puriya Pongukindrathu en asai endru irainji Pogamartha poonmulaiyinal potra Deva Devanum athu thiruvullam seithu thentisai mikka seithavathal Yavarum thanai adaivathu manmel endrum ullathu Kanchi matru athanul Ma amarnda nam irukkaiyil anainthu mannu poosanai magizhndhu seivai endru eva Emperumattiyum piria isaivu kondu ezhundarutharku isainthal." Thus are the 52nd and 53rd verses. Now, their meaning. "Nangai" means the best among women. The Lord (Nayagan) observes the love that arose in the heart of such a Nangai, Ambika. "Nayagan" means the best among men. So, Lord Shiva, observing the love that arose and filled the heart of Ambika, the Nangai, rejoiced in His divine heart, and with a smile appearing on His divine face, asked, "What is in your heart?" To this, the Empress replied, "O Lord, my desire overflows to worship You according to the nature of the Agama that You have expounded here." The phrase "the nature of the Agama that You have expounded" appears. The "nature" referred to here is the performance of Shiva Puja. Performing Shiva Puja is the natural state for souls; everything else is a deviation. This is the implication. Thus, she says, "My heart desires to worship You according to the Agama; I yearn for it greatly." Who is that Empress? Sekkizhar Peruman refers to the Empress in this verse as "Pogamartha Poonmulaiyinal." "Pogamartha Poonmulaiyinal" means one whose breasts are adorned with ornaments that nourish souls with enjoyments. Thirugnanasambandar Peruman, in his Thevaram, Thirunallar Thevaram, begins that Thirunallatru Thirupathikam by praising Ambika with the phrase "Pogamartha Poonmulaiyal." "Pogamartha Poonmulaiyal thannodum pon agalam Pagamartha paingann vellatru annal parametti Agamartha thol udaiyan kovana adaiyin mel Nagamartha nambiraman meyathu nallare" is the first verse of Thirunallatru Thirupathikam. In this, Thirugnanasambandar Peruman has sung of Ambika as "Pogamartha Poonmulaiyal." Thirunallar is a sacred place located in the Chola country. Here, the Lord's name is Darbharanyesvarar, and the Goddess's name is Pogamartha Poonmulaiyal. Thirugnanasambanda Murthigal begins this Thirupathikam with this divine name. In the history of Thirugnanasambandar Peruman, he went to Madurai, cured the fever of Koon Pandiyan, and then engaged in Anarvadam and Punarvadam with the Jains. Anarvadam means lighting a fire and placing our Thirugnanasambandar Peruman's pathikam, written on a palm leaf, into that fire. Similarly, the Jains would write their mantras on palm leaves and place them in the fire. The one whose leaf remained unburnt in the fire would be declared the winner.

Thus, the Anarvadam was concluded. At that time, Thirugnanasambandar Peruman praised Lord Shiva, declared that our Lord Shiva is the Supreme Being, and worshipped Him. He untied the string binding the palm leaf in the Thirumurai and, with his divine hand, marked a line on that Thirumurai. The pathikam that came at that time was the Thirunallatru Thirupathikam, beginning with "Pogamartha Poonmulaiyal" as mentioned above. Thirugnanasambandar Peruman worshipped Lord Shiva, took that leaf from the leaves of the Thirumurai into his divine hand, and to make everyone understand that Paramasivan is the ultimate truth, he sang the Thirupathikam "Thalarila Valaroli" and then, for everyone to see, placed this Thirunallatru Thirupathikam into the fire. After the appointed time, when they looked, the Jains' leaf had turned to ashes, while the divine leaf placed by Thirugnanasambanda Murthi Swamigal shone green. Therefore, this pathikam, beginning with "Pogamartha Poonmulaiyal," came to be known as "Pachai Pathikam" (Green Hymn). Thus, in the Pandya country, Thirugnanasambandar Peruman, who re-established the Saiva religion by singing this Thirupathikam, sang of Ambika as "Pogamartha Poonmulaiyal" in the first line of the first verse of that Thirupathikam. Sekkizhar Peruman has used that phrase here. At this point, which shows that the ultimate conclusion of Saivism, the ultimate truth of Saivism, is Shiva Puja, Sekkizhar Peruman uses the phrase "Pogamartha Poonmulaiyal" to recall this Thirupathikam, which helped establish Saivism and demonstrated it to the world. We should also note here that the phrase is used to indicate the feminine form, the divine body of grace, of the Goddess. "Pogamartha Poonmulaiyal" means Goddess Uma, whose breasts are adorned with ornaments that nourish souls with enjoyments. "Pogam artha" means nourishing souls with enjoyments. "Poonmulaiyal" means Goddess Uma, whose breasts are adorned with ornaments. The two breasts of Ambika are revered as the two states of knowledge, Para and Apara. The conclusion of Saiva Siddhanta is that these two types of knowledge yield the fruit of Shiva's enjoyment. In the Skanda Purana, the breasts of Goddess Uma are referred to as "Paramabodha Neerathai" (filled with supreme knowledge). This is mentioned in the 23rd verse of the Saravana Padalam, Utpatti Kandam, of the Kanda Puranam composed by Kachiyappa Sivachariyar. That 23rd verse is as follows: "Adhi Nayakan Karunaiyai Amalamai Paramabodha Neerathai Irundathan Kongaiyil Pozhi Pal Ethilathathor Kurumani Vallal Meethu Etru Kadhal Ma Magarku Anbinal Aruthinal Gauri." This verse is set in the context when Lord Muruga incarnated and was growing up with the Karthigai women in Saravana Poigai, and Lord Shiva and Goddess Parvati went on their Rishabha vehicle to bestow grace upon Lord Muruga. The meaning of this verse is that Goddess Gauri, with love, offered the milk that flowed from her breasts, which were full of the grace of the primal Lord Shiva, pure and in the form of supreme knowledge, in a beautiful golden bowl to her beloved great son, Lord Muruga, and made him drink it. In this verse, Kachiyappa Sivachariyar has referred to the milk flowing from Ambika's breasts as the grace of God and the form of knowledge. Knowledge is of two types: Para Jnana and Apara Jnana. The meaning here is that Ambika's two breasts are the states of knowledge for these two types of knowledge, Para Jnana and Apara Jnana. Therefore, in this verse, where Ambika, intending to perform Shiva Puja according to the Agamas, presents her request to God, Sekkizhar Peruman has composed it as "Pogamartha Poonmulaiyinal" so that the meaning that Ambika performs Shiva Puja solely to impart knowledge to souls is implicitly conveyed. Lord Shiva listens to this with a smile. Because He had imparted the Agamas to Ambika. All souls in this world are going to receive the benefit of that instruction. Due to the penance that Ambika is about to perform, due to the Shiva Puja, all souls in the world are going to receive the benefit of the instruction given by Lord Shiva. Therefore, the Lord smiles. Now, Lord Shiva, the great God, the God of gods, the chief of all gods, takes Ambika's request into His divine heart and speaks to Ambika as follows: "The southern direction is endowed with great merit from penance. It performed great penance. Therefore, by the merit of that penance, everyone will reach the southern direction of that Bharatadesa. In that southern direction, which performed such great penance, the city of Kanchi remains eternally. In that Kanchi, under the mango tree, we reside with pleasure. Go to that abode and perform Shiva Puja with great joy." Thus, Lord Shiva commands. The Lord refers to the southern direction as "the southern direction that performed great penance." We have contemplated this in the beginning of Periya Puranam, in the Thirumalai Sarga, in the section on the glory of Thirumalai, in the 35th verse. In that verse, "Madhavam seitha thentisai vazhnthida theethila thiruthondar thokai thara pothavar mel manam pokkida kadhal madharum katchiyil kanninar," we contemplated all the explanations for "the southern direction that performed great penance." Recalling all that here, when describing the glories of such a southern direction that performed great penance, Sekkizhar Peruman also mentioned Kanchipuram there. At that time, we saw the explanations that Kanchipuram is a Prithvi Sthalam filled with Shakti. Therefore, we should recall all that and contemplate it here. Furthermore, we can contemplate here all the glories of Kanchipuram from the Kanchi Puranam that we have just seen in Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. "Ka" means Brahma, and "anjuthal" means desiring, thus meaning the place desired by Shiva, who is Brahma. Or, taking "Ka" as Brahma Deva and "anjuthal" as reaching, it means the place where Brahma Deva reached and performed puja. Just as the ornament "Kanchi" is worn at the navel of a woman, so too Kanchipuram is located at the navel of the woman that is the earth. Therefore, it is named Kanchi. We saw all this in the summary of the Thiruvekamba Padalam in Kanchi Puranam. We also contemplated that in such a Kanchipuram, the Vedas themselves stood as a mango tree, and the Lord appears beneath it. That mango tree embodies the four Vedas and yields four kinds of fruits. Lord Shiva says, "We reside under such a glorious mango tree." He tells the Goddess, "Go there and perform Shiva Puja with joy." When Lord Shiva says this, He says, "Mannu poosanai magizhndhu seivai" (Perform the lasting puja with joy). "Mannu poosanai" means lasting puja. The Shiva Puja performed by our Empress is a lasting Shiva Puja, a Shiva Puja that will continue forever. Furthermore, in Shiva Puja, both the souls who worship and the God who is worshipped are eternal. "Were you not my servant then?" is the saying of Thayumanavar Swamigal. According to that, even in the state of liberation, souls will serve Shiva. Thus, the Goddess performs the lasting puja so that souls may eternally worship God and attain salvation. This is the implication here. When the Lord said this, Goddess Uma, though unwilling to be separated from the Lord, nevertheless, according to the Lord's command, prepares to depart from Kailash and go to Kanchi. To be continued.

Chapter 18 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 226. Mummaiyal Ulaganda Sarga, Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. According to Lord Shiva's divine command, Ambika departs from Mount Kailash for Kanchipuram to perform Shiva Puja. The 54th verse is as follows: "Ethamil pala yoni enbathu nangu noorayiram thannul vaitha Bethamum purantharum a karunai piran mozhintha agama vazhi peni Podhu neermaiyil thozhuthana pothap poruppil vendhanum viruppil vandheithi Madhavam purintharuthal amaintha valathodum parisanangalai viduthan." This is that verse. Its meaning: Goddess Uma, following the path of the Agamas expounded by Lord Shiva, who is endowed with compassion that protects and nurtures all beings placed in the 84 hundred thousand (8.4 million) types of wombs (yoni bedhas) to remove their impurities, proceeds with devotion, worshipping the Lord. Just as when Parvati Devi incarnated as the daughter of Himavan and performed penance towards Lord Shiva, Himavan provided Parvati Devi with the materials for puja and maidservants, similarly now, with puja materials carried by maidservants accompanying her, Ambika departed from Mount Kailash. This is the meaning of that verse. First, Sekkizhar Peruman mentions "Ethamil." "Etham" means fault or blemish. The puja performed by our Empress is for the purpose of nullifying the bondage of Anava Mala that binds souls, to liberate souls from the bondage of Anava Mala. Thus, to liberate souls from the bondage of Anava Mala that has eternally gripped them, God bestows grace by causing them to be born in various types of wombs according to their karmas. Thus, Lord Shiva bestows such grace so that souls, born in various types of wombs, experience karmas, exhaust karmas, and thereby sever the bondage of Anava Mala and attain liberation. Therefore, Lord Shiva arranges for souls to be born in 8.4 million types of wombs according to their karmas. Generally, our shastras state that souls have seven births and four types of origins. The seven types of births are: gods, humans, animals, birds, reptiles, aquatic beings, and plants. Our shastras state that there are a total of 8.4 million types of wombs for these seven births. Among these 8.4 million types of wombs, gods have 1.1 million types, humans 0.9 million, quadrupeds 1 million, birds 1 million, reptiles 1.5 million, aquatic beings 1 million, and plants 1.9 million. Thus, the soul is born in these 8.4 million types of wombs, in these seven types of births, and experiences the karmas associated with them, according to our shastras. In the Thirukkural, in the chapter "Puthalvaraip Peruthal" (Having Children), Parimelazhagar, in his commentary on the Kural "Ezhupirappum theeyavai theenda pazhipiranga panpudai makkad perin" (If one gets children of good character who are not blamed, evil will not touch them in seven births), states that "Ezhupirappum theeyavai theenda" means that the seven births that occur due to karma. In such seven births, troubles will not reach a person. How? If he gets children of good character who are not blamed by others, then troubles will not approach him in all seven types of births. In his commentary, he specifically quotes an old Tamil poem that speaks of these 8.4 million types of wombs in these seven types of births. "Oorva onram onpathu manidam neer paravai narkal or pappathu see Piriya panthamam devar 14 ayan padaitha anthamil seer thavaram nalainthu enpathu" is that poem. Furthermore, Thirugnanasambandar Peruman has expressed the same idea in the fourth verse of the Thiruveezhimizhalai Thirupathikam in the First Thirumurai. He sings: "Uraiserrum enbathu nangu noorayiramam yonibhedam Niraisera padaithu avatrin uyirkku uyirai angange nindran koyil Veraiserrum mukin muzhava mayilgal pala nadamada vandu pada Viraiserrum pon ithazhi thara menkanthal kaiyerkum Mizhalaiyame." In the first line, he sings about Lord Shiva: "Uraiserrum enbathu nangu noorayiramam yonibhedam Niraisera padaithu avatrin uyirkku uyirai angange nindran." The meaning of this is that Lord Shiva systematically created 8.4 million differences in birth, known as yoni bedhas, and stands within them as the life of those lives. Thus, many of our shastras and great sages have sung about the 8.4 million yoni bedhas. Thus, Ambika departs to perform Shiva Puja according to the Agamas revealed by the compassionate Lord Shiva, who causes souls to be born in 8.4 million yoni bedhas to experience their karmic actions. At that time, attendants also accompany Ambika. This is similar to how, when our Empress once incarnated as Parvati Devi, the daughter of Himavan, and began to perform penance, her father Himavan provided Parvati Devi with the necessary materials for Shiva Puja and maidservants. He received grace by doing so. Similarly, now, as Ambika departs from Mount Kailash for Kanchipuram, maidservants carrying the materials for Ambika's Shiva Puja and other necessities also depart with Ambika. Ambika performs Shiva Puja for the salvation of souls. Therefore, various souls, knowing that Ambika is coming to perform Shiva Puja for their salvation, accompany Ambika on her journey. The Goddess comes to perform Shiva Puja with compassion for souls in her heart. Therefore, many souls accompany the Empress to receive the benefits of the Shiva Puja performed by the Goddess. How Ambika departed from Mount Kailash and came to Kanchipuram, and how all souls accompanied Ambika, are described in verses 112 to 121 of the Thazhuvakkuzhaintha Padalam in Kanchi Puranam. Sivakavimani Ayya writes in his commentary, "See that." Accordingly, our Empress, who resides with Lord Shiva on Mount Kailash, rises from that seat, bows at the Lord's feet, and receives His consent. Having thus received the Lord's consent to go to Kanchipuram and perform Shiva Puja, she begins to walk from there. On one side, there is the sorrow of leaving Lord Shiva from Mount Kailash. On the other side, there is the joy of having to worship Ekambaranathar in Kanchipuram. The sorrow of leaving Mount Kailash pulls Ambika back. But the joy of having to worship her Lord Ekambaranathar in Kanchipuram pulls Ambika forward towards Kanchipuram. Therefore, Ambika bows to Lord Shiva and repeatedly prostrates at the Lord's feet, saying, "O Lord, wherever I am, You must bestow Your grace," and then stands up and repeatedly says, "I am going, I am going," worshipping the Lord, taking leave, and walking slowly. She walks like a creeper gently swaying. As she departs from Mount Kailash in this manner, she walks backward slowly, looking at Lord Shiva's divine face, without showing her back to Lord Shiva.

She comes, bowing to Lord Shiva. After visiting the temple and completing the darshan of the Lord, we too should walk backward, looking at the Lord's divine face, without showing our back to the Lord. This is a rule for visiting Shiva temples. The Goddess reminds us of this here. Our Empress, who shines as the boundless, perfect, blissful, luminous light, seen by great ascetics and yogis who have subdued the three impurities of desire, anger, and delusion, and who have controlled the five sensory hunters of body, mouth, eyes, nose, and ears, and escaped their plunder, walks backward, looking at Lord Shiva's divine face, so that Lord Shiva appears before her divine eyes. Walking thus, she crosses the first gopuram gate of Mount Kailash, and continues to walk backward until Lord Shiva's divine face is visible to her eyes. After crossing the first gopuram gate, she begins to walk forward towards Kanchipuram. With difficulty, she parts from Mount Kailash, unwilling to leave, and by the divine grace of Lord Shiva, she ascends and sits on a great luminous vimana carried by many bhoota ganas. Thus seated, she begins her journey towards Kanchipuram. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 19 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 227, Mummaiyal Ulaganda Sarga, Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. From Mount Kailash, having taken leave of the Lord, Ambika ascends and sits in a great luminous vimana. Ambika's retinue of female companions also ascend and sit in that vimana. Thus seated, Ambika, with her companions, travels in the vimana towards Kanchipuram. At that time, with supreme joy, every soul accompanies Ambika on that journey, thinking that our Mother, the Empress, is going to perform puja to Thiruvekambanatha according to Agama rules. Auspicious sounds are heard everywhere. Rudra Kannikas, with boundless joy, make joyous sounds and gather in crowds, surrounding and walking with Ambika's vimana. Lakshmi Devi, who resides in the red lotus, also joins. Saraswati Devi also joins, surrounded by crores of women on her side. Indrani, with her female companions, joins Ambika. Surrounded by countless women, the Sapta Matrikas – Brahmi, Maheswari, Kaumari, Narayani, Varahi, Indrani, and Kali – also join. Among the celestial women, crores of women from Garuda, Gandharva, Siddha, and Vidyadhara also join Ambika and journey to Kanchipuram. The deities Dakini, Kali, and Yogini, wielding tridents, also join with their retinues. Rishi Patnis also join Ambika. Thus, everyone joins Ambika and begins the journey. All these women who joined serve Ambika in various ways as they come. Furthermore, Uragar, Gandharvar, Rakshasar, Yakkar, Rudrar, Maruthar, Vasukkal, Garudar, Kimpurushar, Charanar, Siddhar, Devar, Brahma, Vishnu, 48,000 munis, and deities of various religions all come, prostrate at the lotus feet of the Empress, and with great joy, chant "Jaya Jaya Potri" and walk ahead. Percussion instruments like tannumai, murudu, kudamuzha, monthai, thaguniccham, perigai, thakkai, pathalai, murasam, etc., are played. Wind instruments like thiruchinnam, ekkalam, kombu, sangu, kuzhal, etc., are blown. Other instruments also play their own sounds. The sounds of these various instruments surpassed the roar of the seven seas. Thus, the roar of the seven seas was subdued by these instrumental sounds. The women accompanying Ambika wave white chamaras inlaid with navaratnas. They hold white silk umbrellas. They carry fans and alavattams here and there, accompanying the Empress. Along their path, Kalpaka trees shower flowers. The women accompanying the Empress sing sweetly like cuckoos. They sing pallandu (long life) for the Empress. The warrior women in the Empress's army, dressed in their masculine attire, walk ahead, wielding shining swords. Miraculous items like kulikai that can transmute the five metals accompany them. The Pancha Tarus (five celestial trees) that bestow abundant wealth also accompany them. All sacred tirthas accompany the Empress. All sacred rivers accompany Ambika. Dwarfs and bhootas walk ahead eagerly and quickly, waving their hands and making "guru guru" sounds. The clanging sounds from various instruments, the "kalakala" sounds, the "kalin kalin" sounds, the "salasal" sounds, the "sadadasa" sounds, and other sounds all resound there. Yet, above all these sounds, "Hara Hara" is heard everywhere. "Jaya Jaya" is heard everywhere. Thus, many sounds reverberate in all eight directions. The chants of the thiruchinnams praising the Empress, who is coming to Kanchipuram to perform penance, also spread everywhere. The chants of the thiruchinnams spread everywhere, proclaiming that she who is united with the Lord of the Vedas in one half has come, that she who is dear to the gods has come, that the great compassionate Empress has come, that the pure supreme light has come, and that the Empress who gave birth to all worlds in ancient times has come to worship Thiruvekambar of Kachi, rich in water. The Empress, who stands by her husband's command, comes like a boundless ocean of overflowing compassion. Ambika comes as if all the penance performed by the southern direction has gathered and is coming. She comes through the sky with boundless joyous adornments. Because Ambika comes through the sky, the sun and other luminaries are obscured, and the radiance of those who come in front of Ambika's procession shines there. The radiance of the bodies of those who walk in front of Ambika's procession appears like many lightning flashes appearing together. Their shouts of praise and "Jaya Jaya" resound like thunder. All of them in the procession walk very closely together. They wear many ornaments made of pure gold. As they walk so closely, the ornaments on their bodies rub against each other, and the clusters of navamani (nine gems) fall down like a rain of navaratnas. All who hear the thiruchinnams raised in praise of Ambika rejoice greatly, thinking that the World Mother is coming. They are filled with joy, immersed in a flood of supreme bliss, shedding tears, standing with folded hands and worshipping. The inhabitants of the earth celebrate, hearing that Ambika is coming to Kanchipuram to perform penance, to perform Shiva Puja, to remove all our birth-related ailments, and that the time for us to attain blissful liberation has come. They rejoiced greatly, thinking in various ways: what penance this earth has performed for Ambika to come here and perform Shiva Puja, what penance this Bharat Khanda has performed, what penance this southern direction in Bharat Khanda has performed, and what penance Kanchipuram has performed. Thus, Ambika approaches Bharat Khanda in a great luminous vimana through the sky. Upon arriving in Bharatadesa, Ambika visits all the sacred places where Lord Shiva graciously resides. Our Empress first descends in Kasi in Bharatadesa, installs a Shiva Linga there, performs puja, and then worships Kasi Viswanathar. But the desire for Kanchipuram pulls Ambika, so she departs from Kasi. Departing south from Kasi, she lovingly performs puja at every sacred place where Lord Shiva resides, and then reaches Thondai Nadu, which is like the beautiful face of the auspicious land of Tamil Nadu, and our Empress arrives at Kanchipuram. In the month of Aippasi, Krishna Paksha, Somavaram (Monday), Ekadasi, Puram Nakshatra, on an auspicious day when the Sun is in Thula Rasi and the Moon is in Kataka Rasi, our Mother, Parasakti, entered Kanchipuram bearing the name Kamakshi Devi. For the prosperity of all worlds, the divine Empress Kamakshi Devi entered the limits of the city of Kanchipuram. Seeing the city of Kanchi, which remains indestructible even at the end of yugas, she rejoiced more and more, clasped her red hands above her head, her whole body thrilled, shed tears of joy like rain, bathed in a tirtha there, made a sankalpa, wore a golden bangle and a turmeric thread on her luminous hands, installed a linga at that spot, and performed puja.

She performed puja. Since Ambika tied a protective thread and performed puja there, that place is called Kappu Tirtham. That linga is called Kappu Lingam. At the place where she wore the navaratna bracelet, she worshipped Sivaparanjothi, known as Kadakeesan, and wore the bracelet on her hands. Those who worship these two lingas installed by Ambika, Kappu Lingam and Kadaka Lingam (Kadakeesam), will be freed from the three impurities, overcome the sufferings of birth, and live in liberation. Our Empress, who has taken me as her own, thus performed puja and praised, and then entered the city of Kanchipuram, which removes the sufferings of birth for those who remember her. Ambika enters the city, delightedly observing the prosperity of Kanchipuram and showering her divine grace. As our Empress thus enters Kanchipuram, the great serpent Padman places her divine feet on his head and pleads, "O Mother, who gave birth to all worlds, please reside in the bilam (underground abode) where I live and establish your temple." There are eight serpents in the eight directions, one for each direction. They are Vasuki, Anantan, Takshan, Sangapalan, Kulikan, Padman, Mahapadman, and Karkotakan. Among them, the great serpent Padman resided in a bilam in Kanchi. It was he who now bowed to Ambika and requested her to reside in that bilam. That bilam is now located inside the Thirukamakottam temple, where Kamakshi Amman resides, which is centrally located in Kanchipuram. According to Padmanaga's request, Kamakshi Devi resided in that bilam. She established her temple there. Now, Ambika desired to perform puja to Lord Shiva, with boundless joy and grace in her heart, for the salvation of all souls on earth. Lord Shiva had already said on Mount Kailash, "We reside under the mango tree in Kanchipuram. Go there and perform puja." Accordingly, Kamakshi Devi now searches for the place where Lord Shiva resides. At that time, Lord Shiva performs a divine play. He hides Himself without appearing to Ambika, who is searching for Him. Is not hiding and appearing the divine play of God? Is not the Lord's hiding in this manner to further increase devotion in Shiva devotees? Therefore, the Lord hides Himself without revealing Himself. So, Ambika, with great love, performed intense penance to see Him. The Goddess, who is the inseparable power of grace from God, thus performs puja as if she is separate from God. The Lord accepts her puja. Thus, the Lord and the Goddess manifest as two. Then, when the Goddess embraces the Lord, they appear as one. Thus, it is one of the unique glories of Kanchipuram that the one Supreme Being Shiva manifests as two, as Ambika and the Lord, and then Ambika and the Lord appear as one again, giving the vision of unity, the unity of Shiva-Shakti. Thus, our Empress searches for the Lord with great love to perform puja to Him. The Lord hides there and performs a divine play. "Nenjam Eesanai Kanpathe Virumbi Nirantharam Thiruvakkinil Nigalvathu Anjezhuthume Aga Aludaiya Ammai Semmalar Kai Kuvithu Irainji Thanjamaghiya Arunthavam Puriya Tharippare Aval Thaniperum Kanavar Vanja Neekkiya Mavvin Moolathin Vanthu Thondrinar Malaimagal Kana" is the next verse composed by Sekkizhar Peruman.

No. As the Goddess yearned, unable to see the Lord, Kamakshi Devi's heart desired only to see the Lord. The sacred Panchakshara always resounded on Ambika's divine tongue. Ambika worshipped with her lotus-like hands clasped together. Since Ambika thought of the Lord in her heart, it signifies the mind; since she chanted the sacred Panchakshara with her divine tongue, it signifies speech; and since she clasped her hands, it signifies the body. Thus, the penance performed with the three instruments of mind, speech, and body, all united in discipline, is described there. Kamakshi Amma performs that penance. Lord Shiva, who resides under the mango tree in the form of the Vedas, was hidden at the root of that mango tree to remove the impurities of souls. The mango tree there is the form of the Vedas. Just as the Vedas keep the Supreme Being Shiva very secretly within their heart, placing the Aksharatvaya (two letters) within the Sri Rudra, and then reveal Him, similarly, Lord Shiva was hidden at the root of that mango tree. But seeing His consort Ambika thinking of Him with her whole heart, chanting the sacred Panchakshara with her divine tongue, and worshipping Him with her lotus-like hands, would He endure any longer? Lord Shiva, her unique great husband, unable to bear it, appeared at the foot of that mango tree for Ambika to see. He gave darshan to Ambika in the form of a Shiva Linga at the foot of that mango tree. Seeing the Lord appear there according to the promise He made in Kailash, "I will give you darshan at the foot of the mango tree in Kanchipuram," Ambika was overwhelmed and bowed to Lord Shiva, her life's Lord. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 20 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 228. Mummaiyaal Ulaganda Sarukkam, Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. In Kanchipuram, the Lord manifested as a Lingam under a mango tree, and the Goddess bowed down to worship Him. Sekkizhar Peruman composed the verse: "Seeing the Lord, the fruit of great penance, who resided in Ekambam, the Goddess, with hair adorned with buzzing bees falling forward as she bowed, her desire surging with love, resolved to perform a specific worship without limit. The fair-mouthed Uma Devi, with lips like a kovai fruit, began her pure worship." The beautiful-mouthed Goddess, whose lips resemble the kovai fruit, thought in her mind that the Lord resides in Ekambam as a result of her great penance. She praised Him with her words and bowed down to Him with a Panchanga Namaskaram. With her hair, bound and buzzing with bees, falling forward, the Goddess performed the Panchanga Namaskaram there. We can see here the sight of her hair falling forward as she bowed, with five limbs touching the ground, as is customary for women. Now, the next verse: "To the worship of the Lord of the heavens, our Empress, understanding the truth of the Agamas He Himself proclaimed, prepared to offer worship. Her maidens, like tender creepers, brought baskets of flowers. She gathered pure, fragrant, fresh flowers from the Ambika Vanam, the sacred garden, avoiding the tender sprouts at their feet." This is the next verse. The Empress has come here to perform Shiva Puja. She advises her maidens to bring the necessary materials for Shiva Puja, understanding the truth of the Agamas that Lord Shiva Himself taught her, and according to those Shiva Agamas. The Goddess herself established a garden there for Shiva Puja. Therefore, that garden is named Ambika Vanam. It is an excellent rule that those who perform Shiva Puja should establish their own gardens for the flowers required for their worship and gather the appropriate flowers themselves. Thus, the garden established by Ambika came to be known as Ambika Vanam. Furthermore, it received the name Ambika Vanam because it had the great fortune of having its flowers plucked by Ambika's own hands. Now, in the next verse, Sekkizhar Peruman sings about how Ambika's maidens assisted her in Shiva Puja. "The Empress, who gave birth to all beings, performed the Shiva Puja according to all the Agamic rites, with the maidens providing all that was needed at the right time: the many flowers gathered, the sacred water from the Kamba River, the fragrant anointing paste, the ghee-laden incense and lamps, and abundant love in her full heart." This is that verse. Ambika's maidens bring the many flowers they have gathered for Ambika to perform Shiva Puja. They bring water from the Kamba River for the sacred ablution. The Kamba River is a river that flows in Kanchipuram. The Kanchi Puranam states that it now flows underground. It is a general Shiva Agamic principle that river water is excellent for sacred ablutions. Now, the maidens bring the ghee-laden incense and lamps to Ambika. Ghee-laden incense is an incense smoke item made by adding ghee to frankincense and other ingredients. We have discussed the method of offering incense according to the Agamas in the Puranam of Kungiliyak Kalaya Nayanar. Thus, Ambika's maidens assist her by bringing the flowers, materials for sacred ablution, materials for anointing Lord Shiva, incense, and lamps required for Ambika's Shiva Puja. It is noteworthy that these five – flowers, sacred water, anointing paste, incense, and lamps – are indispensable for Shiva Puja. With all these, Ambika performs Shiva Puja according to the Agamas. The Kanchi Puranam elaborates on how Ambika performed Shiva Puja there according to the Agamas, as follows: Ambika, about to begin Shiva Puja, instructs her maidens to keep providing her with the necessary items for the puja. As soon as they heard the divine words of the Goddess, her maidens were overjoyed. They fell at the lotus feet of the Goddess, bowed down, and then said to the Goddess, "Please tell us individually the services we need to perform." Thus, the maidens of the Goddess stood requesting her. Then the Goddess said to her maidens as follows: "Maidens, I must bathe and perform my daily rituals without fail. Therefore, create a well suitable for that. Create a well for the anointing of our Lord Shiva, Thiruneelakanta Peruman." Thus, the Goddess said to some of her maidens. And to some other maidens, the Goddess said, "Cultivate gardens on the banks of the Vegavathi River. The banks of the Vegavathi River will be filled with flowers, green leaves, and fruits. Cultivate gardens with many trees like Mayilai, Vilvam, Pala, and so on." Thus she said. And to some other maidens, she said, "Bring fragrant sandalwood paste, kalabak kasturi, green camphor, saffron, and other fragrant substances, divine gem ornaments, priceless and invaluable parivattam, and so on." Thus, the Goddess said to some. And to some other maidens, she said, "Bring Panchagavya along with Panchamirtham." And to some other maidens, she said, "Decorate the temples and mansions of Kanchipuram everywhere with navamanis, beautiful flowers, and embroidered silk cloths." Thus, the Empress explained to her maidens individually how to make arrangements for Shiva Puja. As soon as the Empress commanded, all the maidens fell at the feet of the Goddess, worshipped, and rose, saying, "We will do it first, we will do it first," each striving in their respective tasks with great love, surpassing one another in speed. Among the maidens of Uma Devi, Umapathrai and Keerthimathi, two maidens, created two wells, one in the north and one in the south, and filled them with sacred waters like Ganga and Kalindhi. Some other maidens, to prepare sacred food for Ekambaranathar, who has matted locks, created a well in the Agni corner and established beautiful kitchens. Some maidens, to adorn Ekambaranathar's matted locks, cultivated gardens called Ambika Vanam on the banks of the Vegavathi River, west of Kanchi city. That Ambika Vanam shone as a glorious garden, pure and worthy of worship by many. Some other maidens arranged a wondrous canopy. That canopy was adorned with long-leaved plantain trees, areca nut trees with fruits that ripened red like fire, and red sugarcane that yields jaggery, tied to each pillar. They hung radiant gem garlands and strung fragrant flower garlands in rows on that canopy.

Some other maidens applied fragrant civet, added pollen powder, sprinkled scented rosewater, raised fragrant agarwood smoke, and crushed gold into powder, dissolved it, and painted all the walls of the beautiful mansion golden. Some other maidens prepared various offerings: fragrant rice mixed with spices, plain rice, sweet pongal, curd rice, and other delicious rice dishes, as well as fried curries seasoned to release their aroma. These offerings, which included four types of food – those that are sucked, licked, bitten, and swallowed – were prepared with six tastes: bitter, pungent, salty, astringent, sour, and sweet. Some maidens gathered honey-laden flowers and wove them into various garlands like Chuttu, Kanni, Indai, Thongal, and placed them in golden baskets, sprinkling rosewater and adding fragrant smoke. Some, chanting divine mantras, filled golden pots with water, tied their mouths with golden ribbons, and poured the purified water, drawn from the surging waves, into many large vessels. Some maidens, on golden plates, purified puja vessels like handbells and arranged them. Some maidens carefully selected all the sacred leaves and flowers, ensuring they were free from worms and insects, and mixed fragrant cardamom and other spices into the sacred water, purifying it with joyful hearts. Some placed clusters of hanging jackfruits, ripe bananas, mangoes, oranges, pomegranates, fragrant lemons, coconuts, and areca nuts there. Some maidens collected cow's milk in many golden pots. Similarly, they brought countless pots of ghee, honey, sugarcane juice, curd, and divine nectar. Some brought beautifully embroidered silk garments, white silk garments from various islands, green silk garments, red silk garments shining like the Indragopa insect, golden silk garments, blue silk garments, and many silk garments from the Pancha Tarukkal. Some brought navaratna crowns, ear ornaments, golden ornaments, headbands, shoulder ornaments that dispel darkness, rings, bracelets, neck chains, and necklaces, all bestowed by the Kalpaka tree. They brought pots filled with a fragrant sandalwood paste, into which civet was squeezed, powdered green camphor was added, and saffron was mixed, all blended and strained. Some brought mirrors, white whisks, moon-shaped umbrellas, fans, sacred ash, camphor, incense, golden gem pots, plates, and many other items for the puja. Some maidens commanded the celestial damsels to play their melodious veenas, to play various musical instruments, and to dance in accordance with the songs, instructing both gods and celestial damsels. Thus, the maidens of the Empress were preparing all the materials required for the Empress's Shiva Puja. Now, Kamakshi Devi, the mother of all living beings, prepares to perform Shiva Puja. We will continue to contemplate this. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 21 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 229. Mummaiyaal Ulaganda Sarukkam, Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. All the maidens of the Empress have prepared all the materials required for the Shiva Puja that the Empress is performing, as we have seen. Now, the Empress prepares to perform the puja. Sekkizhar Peruman explains this in the following verse: "Understanding that this is the fruit of having hands, with a heart filled with constant love for the Lord of the heavens residing in Ekambam, the mountain daughter, the bestower of boons, whose peerless love blossomed, bowed her head in worship. Her daily puja grew in acceptance by the Lord's divine will." This is the verse composed by Sekkizhar Peruman. Its meaning is as follows: Uma Devi, the mountain daughter, the bestower of boons to beings, performs Shiva Puja. Understanding that the benefit of having hands is to perform Shiva Puja, Ambika, whose heart is filled with great love for Lord Shiva, the God of gods residing in Ekambam, performs Shiva Puja there. As a result, all good things flourished. The puja performed by Ambika, the puja performed with her head bowed in great love, grew in acceptance by Lord Shiva's divine will day by day. This is the explicit meaning of this verse. Now, let us see the commentary. Ambika performs Shiva Puja, understanding that "this is the fruit of having hands." Why did the Lord give hands to living beings? It is so that all living beings may attain Him by performing Shiva Puja. Lord Shiva gave hands to living beings, especially to humans, so that they may perform Karanyasam and perform Shiva Puja to the Lord with their hands. Thirunavukkarasar Peruman sings, "The Lord who gave a mouth to praise, a mind to think, and a head to bow." The Lord created mouths for living beings to praise Lord Shiva. He created minds to think of Lord Shiva. He gave heads to living beings to bow down to Lord Shiva. Thirunavukkarasar Peruman has blessed us with a separate Thiruppathikam called Thiruvangamalai. In it, starting with "Kankal Kanminoh," he has illustrated how each part of the body should be used to worship Lord Shiva. In it, he has blessed us with the divine words, "Join hands and worship, offering great flowers; join hands and worship." Thirunavukkarasar Peruman has said that one should take many flowers with one's hands and offer them as worship in Shiva Puja. Furthermore, he emphasizes the necessity of performing Shiva Puja with hands by saying, "The hand that does not seek and worship is a stone embedded in the earth." In the 42nd verse of Ponvanna Thanthathi by Kalaritrarivar Nayanar, the objective of the Shiva Puja performed by the Mother is for all living beings to attain good and liberation. That is why Sekkizhar Peruman has described the Mother as "Ulagai Aludaiyal," "Pennin Nallaval," "Bhogamartha Poonmulaiyinal," "Ulagu Anaithaiyum Eendraal," and "Man Ulagathu Uyir Thazhaippa Uyirkal Yavaiyum Eendra Empiratti." We can recall all these verses here. The essence of Ambika's Shiva Puja is that living beings should attain Lord Shiva and experience bliss. Ambika possesses Maya Shakti. Therefore, using her Maya Shakti, she provides suitable bodies and senses to living beings, enabling them to know and worship the Lord, and thus all living beings experience bliss with Lord Shiva. The Mother and other living beings are the Nayakis (heroines) who experience the Lord. The Lord Himself is the Anma Nayakan (soul-hero). It is the Mother who enables all living beings, as Nayakis, to experience bliss. This is its meaning. Now, Sri Madhava Sivagnana Swamigal describes the Shiva Puja performed by Kamakshi Devi in the Kanchi Puranam as follows: We have seen in detail how Ambika's maidens made all the necessary arrangements for Ambika's Shiva Puja. With the materials for Shiva Puja and other arrangements ready, Parashakti, the ocean of great compassion, prepares to perform Shiva Puja there. We saw that there is a well created by Umapathrai, one of the Empress's maidens. Now, the Empress goes to that well called Umapathrai. The Empress meditates on the lotus feet of Parameswara Murthy, who shines as the boundless, complete, blissful light. Meditating on the Lord, she bathes in that well, strictly adhering to the Vedic and Agamic rules. Then, she dries her divine body with the cloth given by her maidens, removing the moisture from her body. Then, she wears two embroidered silk garments and then smears sacred ash all over her body. She applies sacred ash as a Tripundram on her forehead. She lovingly wears a Rudraksha garland and completes her daily rituals. With an overflowing desire to worship her Lord, and with her maidens carrying the necessary items and walking around her on both sides, the Empress prepares for Shiva Puja. Everyone, knowing that the Empress is ready to perform Shiva Puja, praises the Goddess and the Lord. Conches, trumpets, drums, ekkalams, thiruchinnams, veenas, flutes, musical pipes, perigais, murasams, and thindimams resound everywhere. Kinnaras, Garudas, and Kimpurushas sing hymns. Celestial maidens and Rudra Kannikas dance. Sages and Brahmins chant the Vedas. The elephant-faced Ganesha Murthy, who holds the five Vedas and six Angas along with the Mahabharata in one hand, is present there, growing with divine power. Subramanya Swami, adorned with a fragrant Kadamba flower garland, is seated there with great joy. Ambika, having pleased Ganesha Murthy in the Niruthi direction and Velayudhapaani (Subramanya Swami) in the Vayu direction with her worship, reaches the sacred entrance where Nandi Devar stands guard. After performing the necessary pujas to the doorkeepers, she sees the Lord who resides gloriously under a mango tree, which has grown and risen as a majestic Vedic form, the Thriyekambam. With the love that arose as soon as her two eyes saw Him, she falls down, bows, rises, and praises the Lord. With a concentrated mind, she chants the Sri Panchaksharam, and with her hands joined above her head, a flood of supreme bliss overflowing, she enters and approaches the Lord, bowing and praising Him many times. Thus, praising the Lord, she saw with her own eyes the wondrous, nectarine grace that spontaneously manifested under a mango tree, emanating the fragrance of knowledge. She absorbed it into her heart, praised Him in various ways, marveled at the Lord's great compassion, was filled with supreme joy, and with tears of bliss flowing all over her divine body, her heart melting, her hair standing on end, her words faltering, the Empress began to perform Shiva Puja, an act of supreme wisdom. The Empress, addressing the Lord, says, "Chandrasekhara, by Your grace, I will perform Your puja without forgetting. Please bestow Your grace, remove all obstacles to Your puja, and You Yourself preside over this puja."

Thus, the Empress makes a sankalpam. She performs Pushpa Shuddhi to Bhuta Shuddhi, then performs Manasa Puja, and purifies the Padhyam, Achamanam, and Arghyam with clear water mixed with fragrant substances, causing her mind to blossom. The Empress praises Sadashiva Murthy, who is seated on the throne, shining as the primordial Shakti. The Empress, with the eye of true knowledge capable of dispelling the darkness of ignorance, clearly sees and praises Ekambaranathar, who is the all-pervading Supreme Light, who shines as the divine lamp in Thriyekambam, and who is the boundless, complete, pure, eternally blissful wealth, without beginning, middle, or end, pervading everywhere. She offers Padhyam at His feet, Achamanam at His divine face, and Arghyam at His divine crown. She offers Madhuvarkam and again offers Achamanam according to the Shiva Agamic rules. She offers the sacred food, cooked with ghee and milk, to the Lord and performs the puja. She applies sacred oil, sacred gooseberry paste, and sacred turmeric paste. She performs ablution with Panchagavya, Panchamirtham, and milk, curd, ghee, honey, and tender coconut water from many pots. She performs ablution with sugarcane juice. She performs ablution with various fruits. She performs sandalwood ablution. The Empress, with the clear water from the well dug by her maiden Keerthimathi, performs a complete ablution to Ekambaranathar according to the rules, chanting the Sri Rudra mantra. Then, she takes a soft silk cloth and gently wipes Lord Shiva's divine body, removing the sacred moisture from His divine body. Then, she adorns the Lord with a red silk garment and a white sacred thread. She adorns Him with ornaments and flower garlands. She offers incense. She offers lamps. She offers the four types of food with six tastes as sacred offerings, then cleanses the Lord's divine hands and coral-red divine mouth with cool water and anoints them. Then, she offers betel leaf and areca nut, chanting mantras, and properly offers incense and lamps. With a joyful mind, she performs the Arathi, a ritual to remove evil eye, circumambulates many times, bows, praises, and completes the Shiva Puja, which she began with immense love, meditating on the Sri Panchaksharam, which remains unwavering in her mind. Thus, Kamakshi Devi, the Empress, has been worshipping Sri Ekambaranathar in this manner for a long time. While the Empress was performing Shiva Puja daily according to the Shiva Agamic rules, on an auspicious day, Ekambaranathar decided to perform a divine play. The Lord decided to enact a divine play filled with love for the Empress. The meaning here is that the Lord performed this divine play, filled with love, to bestow grace upon the Mother and out of His immense love for her. This love that the Lord had for Ambika should not be compared or thought of as the love that we, as living beings (cows), have. The love that we, bound by imperfections, have is a love connected to attachment. To think that the Lord and the Goddess had love like this is a Shiva Aparadham (offence against Shiva). Because the love that the Lord and the Goddess had signifies the state of Tattva, where they are united with Shakti to uplift and save living beings. Living beings have love for themselves, but the Lord undertaking this divine play filled with love is for the sake of living beings, for their salvation. This is its meaning.

We will continue to contemplate the divine play that the Lord performed with the Empress. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 22 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 230. Mummaiyaal Ulaganda Sarukkam, Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. In Kanchipuram, Ambika performs Shiva Puja daily according to the Shiva Agamas. At that time, the Lord desires to perform a divine play with the Empress. Now, the next verse: "Seeing the flood of grace of the Lord, the fish-eyed one, fearing that a great flood would come upon her Lord from the heavens, with an inner trembling love, embraced the Lord of the flower-adorned tresses, who was like herself, preventing the flood with her divine hand." This is the next verse. Ambika is peerless; there is no one equal to her. The Lord is incomparable; similarly, the Goddess is also incomparable, having no equals or superiors. The Empress, as before, bathed in the Kamba River, adorned her body with Rudraksha garlands and sacred ash, completed her daily rituals, and began Shiva Puja, without any deviation in her Shiva service. When she was performing the sacred ablution to Ekambaranathar, filled with Shiva's essence, Lord Shiva, who resided under the mango tree blossoming with the fragrance of knowledge, decided to bestow His grace upon the Empress through a compassionate divine play. He willed that all the sacred waters from countless universes, both within and without, should unite and come forth. As soon as the Lord willed it, all those waters mingled with the Kamba River, and a flood surged forth from the Kamba River, like the deluge that comes during the time of cosmic dissolution. The Devas, the Ashtadikpalakas (guardians of the eight directions), and the inhabitants of the earth, who were present in all the universes, cried out in fear, thinking that the end of the age had come. Even the tears that arose from their cries mingled there as tributary rivers. Thus, the Kamba River, united with the waters of all worlds, began to swell with great speed. The river flood rose to touch the sky. That river flood surged forth like Vishnu, who once incarnated as a boar and lifted the earth. That flood leaped into the sky like Brahma, who once transformed into a swan and searched for the Lord's divine crown. The flood that came in the Kamba River carried along bananas, jackfruits, mangoes, coconuts, agarwood, saffron, sandalwood, flowers, honey, and sugarcane pieces. This was as if the maidens were carrying the items needed for Parvati Devi's Agamic puja. Seeing that flood, which carried along mountain treasures like sandalwood trees, saffron trees, honey, and other substances, as well as many gems, surging with waves, roaring with sound, covering the entire sky, and rising thickly upwards, the Goddess, whose face was like the full moon born of the mountain king, was startled and perturbed. With her maidens surrounding her on both sides also terrified, she looked at the relentlessly approaching flood and wondered, "What is the reason for this flood, which is like the surging ocean, suddenly appearing in the midst of Shiva Puja?" The Empress thought, "Perhaps this flood has come as an obstacle to my puja due to my fault," and feeling distressed, she meditated on the lotus feet of Lord Shiva, Chandrasekhara. But before she could meditate, the great flood had approached very rapidly and was very near. So, filled with distress, she looked at it, trembled with fear, and stood bewildered, thinking, "Oh, this great flood, reaching the sky, will surely come upon the Lord! What can I do now?"

Indeed, the entire golden body of Ambika, whose mind was overflowing with unwavering devotion to the Lord, trembled. Beads of sweat appeared on the forehead of the Goddess, adorned with a tilak. The pearl necklaces resting on her lotus bud-like breasts swayed. The Empress's slender waist became very weak and wilted. She intended to stop the flood with her divine hands. But the flood could not be stopped. The act of the Goddess trying to stop the flood with her hand implies that she, being the causal power of the universe, believed she had the ability to stop it herself. However, the fact that the flood did not stop but surged even more is explained as having happened by the grace of the Lord. That is why Sekkizhar Peruman first states here, "the flood of the Lord's grace." The Empress worried that the flood, which had approached very closely, would surge over the divine body of Ekambaranathar, whom she was worshipping. What to do now? She stood bewildered. Her devotion to the Lord intensified and soared. Her divine body trembled. Small drops of sweat appeared on her forehead. Pearl necklaces swayed on her breasts. Her slender waist wilted. Buzzing bees, immersed in honey, hummed in her hair. The Mekala ornament around her waist jingled. Golden anklets and bells jingled. Golden bracelets on her hands clinked. Like a flowering creeper that sprouted and sent out two tender shoots on either side, embracing a golden mountain peak, the Goddess, like a statue of emerald, quickly rose. She knelt on her right knee on the fragrant altar, and with both her hands, she tightly embraced her Lord, her husband. As soon as the Goddess embraced Him tightly with her mountain-like breasts and her red hands adorned with bangles, with overflowing, endless love, Ekambaranathar, the Lord, revealed His wondrous, blissful, luminous divine body, which dispels the darkness of ignorance, bearing the marks of the bangles on the Empress's red hands and the marks of her breasts. How wondrous, wondrous, wondrous it is! Our Lord, whose divine body is the supreme, blissful light, who is beyond form, formless, atomic, mountainous, existent, non-existent, darkness, light, and everything else, pervading everywhere, bore the marks of the bangles on the red hands and the marks of the breasts of the Goddess, whose hair was adorned with fragrant flowers. How astonishing! Our Lord, whose divine body is the supreme, blissful light, who resides in the mind as the size of a thumb, and who effortlessly moves all the universes, bore the marks of the bangles on the red hands and the marks of the breasts of the Goddess, who possesses great compassion and a gentle smile. How astonishing! Our Lord, whose divine body is the supreme, blissful light, whom Shiva yogis, who can control the Rechaka and Puraka breaths and sleep without sleeping, behold with their eye of true knowledge, bore the marks of the bangles on the red hands and the marks of the breasts of the Goddess, who speaks sweetly like a female cuckoo. How wonderful! My Father, whose divine body is the supreme, blissful light, who is always of one nature, free from thought and forgetfulness, immersed in the Self, and easily accessible to the love of Jnanis, bore the marks of the bangles on the red hands and the marks of the breasts of the Goddess, who has tender, sprout-like small feet. How astonishing! By merely thinking of His divine feet in the mind, the Lord, whose divine body is the supreme, blissful light, casts away the great karmic births of those who think of Him. How wonderful that He bore the marks of the bangles on the red hands and the marks of the breasts of the Goddess, the mother of all worlds. It is a great wonder that the red coral mountain, which bent the golden mountain as a bow, the eternal, beginningless, and endless Vedic form, became soft and slender due to the embrace of a flowering creeper's red hands and breasts. As soon as Thiruekambamudaiyar (Ekambaranathar) bore the marks of the bangles on His red hands and the marks of the breasts on His divine body, overflowing with compassion and attaining supreme bliss, all living beings in the heavens and on earth rejoiced greatly. The earth and the heavens shone without blemish. Countless musical instruments resounded. The gentle breeze wafted with a new fragrance. The dust settled throughout the ocean-bound world. The homa fire rose in a swirling column, radiating light. A shower of golden flowers fell incessantly. The Vedas, with their many meters, all rose individually, rejoiced, and resounded. All the worlds, without suffering from delusion, rejoiced. As all this was happening, the Devas, led by Indra, and the Asuras, all came there with their consorts, overjoyed. Brahma, seated on a lotus, came there with Saraswati. Vishnu, adorned with a cool, fragrant Tulasi garland, came there with Lakshmi. They came rejoicing, saying, "We have received supreme bliss today." The glorious Narada Muni and other Devarishis, and the Brahmarishis led by Vasishta, came in countless joyful crowds, with their tridents and other staffs, and their matted locks shining. Many Shaktis, the daughters of Diti, Aditi, and Daksha, Yoginis, Arundhati, Lopamudra, Anasuya, and other women, all came. The four great sages, Sanaka, Sanatkumara, Sanandana, and Sanatana, came. Agastya Muni, Durvasa Muni, Upamanyu Muni, the heroic Bhutaganas, Rudras, Ganadhipathis, Thirunandi Devar, and everyone else gathered there. Thus, everyone gathered there and continuously beheld the wondrous, luminous, blissful divine body of Ekambaranathar, who had softened and yielded to the Empress's embrace, and they roared "Hara Hara, Jaya Jaya." They plunged into the ocean of supreme bliss, rolled and tossed, and with their eyes, which had attained a great, rare fortune, their flower-like eyes shed tears of joy. Their bodies were covered with goosebumps due to the overflowing love, and they mingled like water absorbed by iron. Reflecting on the great compassion of the Lord's divine grace, their five senses were immersed in bliss. With their hands joined above their heads, Vishnu, who holds the chakra shining like a thousand suns, and Brahma, regretted that they did not have a thousand eyes to behold this wondrous sight. Indra, whose entire body had a thousand eyes, also wept, saying that even these thousand eyes were not enough. In this manner, everyone bowed down, rose, danced in joy, and praised and blessed with a roar like the seven oceans. At that time, only Lord Shiva, the deity adorned with the Konrai flower, knew the supreme bliss they experienced. Who else could know it? At that moment, Lord Paramashiva, who is difficult for the Trimurtis – Brahma, Vishnu, and Rudra – to behold, appeared from the beautiful, luminous Lingam, shining with the wondrous divine body bearing the marks of the Goddess's bangles on her red hands and the marks of her saffron-smeared breasts. The Lord appeared from the beautiful, luminous Lingam. With a gentle smile that radiated moonlight, the Lord established the great flood that came in the Kamba River there by the name of Sarva Teertham. The Lord said to the Empress, who had embraced Him, "See, I am supremely pleased because you have embraced Me." The Empress, the sole sovereign, her two eyes filled with joy at the wondrous divine body of her Lord, released her tightly embracing hands, rose from the earth, and with overflowing love, made an anjali. She plunged into the boundless ocean of supreme bliss, which surged as she beheld the pure, radiant, gem-like divine body, and experienced the bliss of Shiva. Then, the Empress praised Lord Shiva. "Salutations to the pure flame of knowledge, whom the tall one (Vishnu) and Brahma could not see. Salutations to the ocean of great grace, who bestowed fearlessness upon me, Your servant. Salutations to the Supreme Being, who is distinct from the 36 Tattvas, from the earth upwards. Salutations, salutations to the divine being who resides under a mango tree, emanating the fragrance of knowledge. Salutations, I, Your servant, have received a fortune that no one has ever received. Salutations to the one who creates, protects, and sustains all the universes in arrays. Salutations to the Lord who performs the Panchakritya Tandavam in the golden hall, with the heroic anklets resounding. Salutations, salutations to the eternal one who resides under a mango tree, emanating the fragrance of abundant flowers. Salutations, salutations to the one who has the eight forms: earth, water, fire, air, ether, moon, sun, and the master. Salutations, salutations to the one who has three eyes: Soma (moon), Surya (sun), and Agni (fire). Salutations to the Gnana Murthy who resides under a mango tree, blossoming with the fragrance of knowledge. Salutations to Lord Neelakanta. Salutations to the apple of my eye. Salutations to the one who bestows great grace even upon those who worship Him forgetfully. Salutations, salutations to the benevolent one who resides under the Sutha tree, which is the form of the Vedas. Salutations to the ancient one who existed before all things that were born first. Salutations to the new one who will be born after all things that will be born later. Salutations to the boundless, complete one whom the ignorant cannot comprehend. Salutations to the pure, conscious Thiruekamban. Shiva Shiva, praise, praise!" Thus, the Goddess, with her fragrant, flower-adorned hair, praised Him with a melting heart and boundless love. The Lord showered His flood of grace there. "Praise to my Father who resides in Ekambam! Praise to the one whose body is half woman, half man!" We will continue to contemplate this. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 23 — Tiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 231. Mummaiyaal Ulaganda Sarukkam, Thiruk Kurippu Tonda Nayanar Puranam. In Kanchipuram, on a day when the Empress was performing Shiva Puja according to the Agamas, the Lord, intending to perform a divine play, caused a great flood to surge in the Kamba River. Unable to stop the surging flood, the Empress embraced Ekambaranathar tightly with her bangle-adorned hands and her divine breasts. Ekambaranathar softened His divine body to be gentle to the Mother's divine breasts and bangle-adorned divine hands.
